\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}\oplus\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}
with
(\widehat{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}})={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}/3.
It is the
weight lattice
W
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
of su(3). The
unit cell is a triangle as in pistachio color. Sites in
W
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
are painted in three colors: red, blue and green as
an illustration of the splitting (
55
).

The site vectors
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\in
W
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
can be written as
B\mathbf{m}
with integer vector
\mathbf{m}=\left(m_{1},m_{2}\right)
and components (
k_{\mathbf{m}})_{i}

expressed as
\sum m_{j}B_{i}^{j}
where
B_{i}^{j}=({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{i}^{j})
defines the characteristic matrix of
W
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
reading explicitly as follows

B_{i}^{j}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{1}^{1}&{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{2}^{1}\\
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{1}^{2}&{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}_{2}^{2}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}&\frac{\sqrt{6}}{6}\\
0&\frac{\sqrt{6}}{3}\end{array}\right)

(47)

with
\det B=1/\sqrt{3}<1.
Due to the duality,
B
is the inverse of the
root matrix
A
. The euclidian length
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}^{2}

of a site vector is computed as
\sum m_{i}\tilde{K}^{ij}m_{j}
with matrix
\tilde{K}=BB^{T}
coinciding with the inverse of the Cartan matrix. By
replacing into (
44
,
47
)
,
we get

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left(x_{\mathbf{n}}\right)_{1}$&$=$&$\sqrt{2}n_{1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}n_{2}$\\
$\left(x_{\mathbf{n}}\right)_{2}$&$=$&$\frac{\sqrt{6}}{2}n_{2}$\end{tabular},\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left(k_{\mathbf{m}}\right)_{1}$&$=$&$\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}m_{1}$\\
$\left(k_{\mathbf{m}}\right)_{2}$&$=$&$\frac{\sqrt{6}}{6}m_{1}+\frac{\sqrt{6}}{3}m_{2}$\end{tabular}

(48)

and in
K=A^{T}A
as well as
\tilde{K}=BB^{T}:

K=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&-1\\
-1&2\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\tilde{K}=\frac{1}{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
1&2\end{array}\right)

(49)

with

\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{n}}^{T}.\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{n}}

=

2\left[\left(n_{1}\right)^{2}+\left(n_{2}\right)^{2}-n_{1}n_{2}\right]

\in

2\mathbb{Z}

\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}^{T}.\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}

=

\frac{2}{3}\left[\left(m_{1}\right)^{2}+\left(m_{2}\right)^{2}+m_{1}m_{2}\right]

\in

\frac{2}{3}\mathbb{Z}

\mathbf{x}_{\mathbf{n}}^{T}.\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}

=

n_{1}m_{1}+n_{2}m_{2}

\in

\mathbb{Z}

(50)

Notice also that substituting
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}=\left(2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)/3
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}=\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)/3
into the linear
combination
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}=\sum m_{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j},

yields the following

\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}=\frac{1}{3}\left(2m_{1}+m_{2}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}\left(m_{1}+2m_{2}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}

(51)

Introducing new integers p,q and congruence classes
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}\in\left\{0,1,2\right\}

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 3
via
2m_{1}+m_{2}=3p+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}
and
m_{1}+2m_{2}=3q+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}},
we rewrite
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}
as

\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}

=

\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}^{0}+\frac{1}{3}\left(2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}\mathbf{-}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}\left(2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}\mathbf{-}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}

\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}^{0}

=

p\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+q\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}

(52)

where
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{m}}^{0}\in
R
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}.
The extra term
\frac{1}{3}\left(2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}\mathbf{-}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}\left(2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 272$}}}\mathbf{-}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 280$}}}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}

. Setting

\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 3,

3.2
The triplet
(\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}})

Using the above results and the construction (

), we find that in

{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times

which splits into nine sheets as follows

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\limits_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}^{\prime}=0}^{3}\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}\right]\times\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}^{\prime}\right]

Each sheet is isomorphic to

of

\textstyle\mathchar 273

A schematic illustration of the 4D lattice

The even self dual lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
is constructed as

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\cup\left[\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right]\cup\left[\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}+2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right]

(58)

corresponding to a three sheet structure, each isomorphic to
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
. The characteristic 4
\times
4
matrices of the three lattices (
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}
) are as follows

\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&0\\
0&A\end{array}\right),\qquad\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}B&0\\

0&B\end{array}\right)

(59)

with determinants

,
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}=1/3
and
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=1.
The induced metrics for the various lattices are defined like
g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}}=(\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}})^{T}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and
g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast}}=(\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}})^{T}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}
as well as
g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}}=(\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}})^{T}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},
they are
given by

g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&K\\
K&0\end{array}\right),\qquad g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&K^{-1}\\
K^{-1}&0\end{array}\right),\qquad g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{2}\\
I_{2}&0\end{array}\right)

(60)

with
\det g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{{\small 3}}}}=3^{2}
,
\det g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{{\small 3}}}}=1/3^{2}
and
\det\left(g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}}\right)=1.
Based on (
59
), and using the construction (
B.14
) of code-CFTs with
c_{\text{{l}/{r}}}=2\mathrm{r\geq 2}
, the code
\mathcal{C}
is
contained into
\left(\mathbb{Z}_{3}\times\mathbb{Z}_{3}\right)^{\mathrm{r}}
and maps to a 4r dimensional lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}
satisfying the inclusions

\underbrace{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\oplus...\oplus\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}}_{\mathrm{r}\text{ {times}}}\text{ }\subset\text{ }\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}\text{ }\subset\text{ }\underbrace{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}\oplus...\oplus\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}}_{2\mathrm{r}\text{ {times}}}\text{ }\subset\text{ }\mathbb{R}^{2\mathrm{r},2\mathrm{r}}

(61)

This lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}
is even and self dual, characterized by the matrices:

\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\oplus_{i=1}^{\mathrm{r}}A&0\\
0&\oplus_{i=1}^{\mathrm{r}}B\end{array}\right),\qquad g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}}^{{\small[2\mathrm{r}]}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{2\mathrm{r}}\\
I_{2\mathrm{r}}&0\end{array}\right)

(62)

with determinants
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=1
and
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=1.
Thus,
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}
defines a Narain theory with central charge 2
\mathrm{r}
.

4
Particles in NCFTs and path to topological matter

The present and following section lay out a proposal to
extend the holographic ensemble framework to discrete lattice QFT with
applications to topological phases of matter featuring Dirac points with
fermionic zero modes
AZ
-
cr1
. In this context, Code and Narain CFTs are imagined as sitting at critical points of
quantum fields
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}\left(\mathbf{r}_{\mathbf{m}}\right)
populating the
sites of the lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}
with position vectors

\mathbf{r}_{\mathbf{m}}=x_{\mathbf{m}}^{a}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{a}
and Fourier
expansions

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}\left(\mathbf{r}_{\mathbf{m}}\right)\simeq\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\mathbf{k}}\mathbf{e}^{i\mathbf{k.r}_{\mathbf{m}}}\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}}_{\mathbf{k}}\qquad,\qquad\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}}_{\mathbf{k}}\simeq\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\mathbf{r}_{\mathbf{m}}}\mathbf{e}^{-i\mathbf{k.r}_{\mathbf{m}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}\left(\mathbf{r}_{\mathbf{m}}\right)

(63)

Here the waves
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 286$}}}\left(\mathbf{r}_{\mathbf{m}}\right)
will be
interpreted as KK particles
\left|KK_{\mathbf{m}}\right\rangle
and
winding states
\left|W_{\mathbf{m}}\right\rangle
sitting in
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}
as illustrated in Figure
12
.

Figure 12:
The 2D even self dual lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}\simeq(\sqrt{2}\mathbb{Z})\times(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathbb{Z})
with red sites occupied by KKs and blue sites by
windings. A red (blue) site has two first closed neighbors of blue (red)
color; this is because
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}
. The area of the
unit cell is equal to 1.

Our proposal borrows ideas from tight binding constructions of particles on
lattices to describe critical properties of KKs and winding states. A more
detailed treatment of this tight binding will be developed in section 5 with explicit computations. Meanwhile, we lay here the groundwork
by studying first the energy and the momentum properties of particle states
in code-CFTs characterised by the triplet (
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast}
) realised in terms of the lattices of SU(2). After that, we
extend this construction to the SU(3) based CFT.

4.1
Particles in SU(2) based model

We now investigate the quantum particles in the code CFT description of the
model with central anomaly c
l
/
r
=1,
corresponding to an SU(2) CS theory in the bulk. It consists of left and
right moving particle states
\left|P_{\text{{l}}},P_{\text{{r}}}\right\rangle
having quantized left/right moving momenta given
by (
A.144
) namely
dec

P_{\text{{l}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]+\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\right)

P_{\text{{r}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]-\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\right)

(64)

These momenta are labeled by two pairs of quantum numbers namely:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the pair
(a,b)
with
a,b=0,...,\mathrm{k}-1
defining
the ground configurations; and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the pair
(n,m)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
encoding the degrees of KK and winding
mode excitations. For convenience, we rewrite these momenta like

\left(P_{\text{{l}}}\right)_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}\bar{a}+\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}\bar{b}\right)

\left(P_{\text{{r}}}\right)_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}\bar{a}-\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}\bar{b}\right)

(65)

where we have set
\bar{a}=a+\mathrm{k}n
and
\bar{b}=b+\mathrm{k}m
(i.e:
\bar{a},\bar{b}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}
). From the above expressions,
several interesting features emerge. In particular, we highlight the
following four:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

Hilbert space of zero modes:

By setting
n=m=0
in (
65
), we find k
2
left and k
2
right
moving ground states with momenta
\left(\mathring{p}_{\text{{l}/{r}}}\right)_{ab}
. They are given by

\left(\mathring{p}_{\text{{l}}}\right)_{ab}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}a+\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}b\right)

\left(\mathring{p}_{\text{{r}}}\right)_{ab}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}a-\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}b\right)

(66)

These can be interpreted as classical charges defining distinct vacuum
states.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

Excitations:

The full momenta in eq(
64
) split into two sectors: the
ground sector defining ground configurations with
\left(\mathring{p}_{\text{{l}/{r}}}\right)_{ab}
and a second sector for the
excitations having momenta
\left(p_{\text{{l}/{r}}}\right)_{nm}
as follows

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$P_{\text{{l}}}$&$=$&$\mathring{p}_{\text{{l}}}+p_{\text{{l}}}$\\
$P_{\text{{r}}}$&$=$&$\mathring{p}_{\text{{r}}}+p_{\text{{r}}}$\end{tabular},\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\left(p_{\text{{l}}}\right)_{nm}$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}n+\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}m\right)$\\
$\left(p_{\text{{r}}}\right)_{nm}$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}n-\sqrt{2}\text{{r}}m\right)$\end{tabular}

(67)

\left(\mathbf{iii}\right)

Energy and momentum
:

Because the left and the right
P_{\text{{l}}/\text{{r}}}
are
linear combinations, the KK and the windings can be decoupled via a change
of variables

H

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(P_{\text{{l}}}+P_{\text{{r}}}\right)

P

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(P_{\text{{l}}}-P_{\text{{r}}}\right)

(68)

with

H

=

\mathring{h}+h

P

=

\mathring{p}+p

(69)

and

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathring{h}$&$=$&$\frac{a}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}$\\
$\mathring{p}$&$=$&$\frac{b}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\sqrt{2}${r}\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$h$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}n}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\frac{1}{\text{{r}}\sqrt{2}}$\\
$p$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}m}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\sqrt{2}${r}\end{tabular}

(70)

\left(\mathbf{iv}\right)

Topological index
:

Using eqs(
64
-
65
), we introduce a ”topological index”
\left(I_{\mathrm{k}}\right)_{\text{{lr}}}
given by
P_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-P_{\text{{r}}}^{2}
which is globally defined on the moduli
space of the NCFT. It factorises like
\left(P_{\text{{l}}}-P_{\text{{r}}}\right)\left(P_{\text{{l}}}+P_{\text{{r}}}\right)
and evaluates to

\left(I_{\mathrm{k}}\right)_{\text{{lr}}}

=

\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}\bar{a}\bar{b}

=

\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}\left(a+\mathrm{k}n\right)\left(b+\mathrm{k}m\right)

(71)

Remarkably, this index is independent of the parameter
r

coordinating the moduli space of NCFT. By substituting
\bar{a}=a+\mathrm{k}n
and
\bar{b}=b+\mathrm{k}m,
this index expands in terms of the CS level
as follows

\left(I_{\mathrm{k}}\right)_{\text{{lr}}}=\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}ab+2\left(am+bn\right)+\left(2\mathrm{k}\right)nm

(72)

Notice that for
\mathrm{k=1,}
it reduces to the usual
\left(I_{\mathrm{1}}\right)_{\text{{lr}}}=2nm\in 2\mathbb{Z}
and for
\mathrm{k=2,}

we have
\left(I_{\mathrm{2}}\right)_{\text{{lr}}}=ab+2\left(am+bn\right)+4nm.
And because
\left(I_{\mathrm{k}}\right)_{\text{{lr}}}
is a globally defined feature, we may fix the parameter

r
for convenience and set for example
r
=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}.

4.1.1
Exhibiting the SU(2) symmetry

The above relations characterising the left/right momenta
P_{\text{{l}/{r}}}
admit a natural lattice description in terms of the SU(2)
root and weight systems. They can be imagined as vectors
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}/{r}}}
valued in the root R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
and weight W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
lattices of SU(2) as
follows

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}/{r}}}\sim n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\pm m\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\quad\sim\quad z_{\pm}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\qquad,\qquad z_{\pm}=2n\pm m\quad\in\quad\mathbb{Z}

(73)

Recall that
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
are respectively generated by the simple
root
\textstyle\mathchar 267
and the fundamental
\textstyle\mathchar 277
satisfying
the duality relation
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=1
with norms
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}=2
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}=1/2
. For simplicity, we
may identify these vectors like
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}=\sqrt{2}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=1/\sqrt{2}
in which case the momenta (
64
) take the form:

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{2\text{{r}}}\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}+2\text{{r}}\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\frac{1}{2\text{{r}}}\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}-2\text{{r}}\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

(74)

Moreover, by setting
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}=\frac{1}{2\text{{r}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=2
r
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}},
the momenta written as

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}+\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}-\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

(75)

with
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}.\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=1
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2\text{{r}}^{2}}
as well as
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{2}=2
r
{}^{2}.
Using
r
=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}},
we
get

\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}=\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{k}}{2}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}.\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=1

(76)

showing that for k=2, we have
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}
and

\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}.
Putting these changes into the
above relationship, we end up with

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}$&$=$&$\frac{a}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}$&$=$&$\frac{b}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{h}$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}n}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}m}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}$\end{tabular}

(77)

and

\boldsymbol{H}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\bar{a}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(a+\mathrm{k}n\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}

\boldsymbol{P}

=

\frac{b}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\bar{b}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}

=

\frac{b}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(b+\mathrm{k}m\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}

(78)

and subsequently

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\bar{a}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}+\bar{b}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}+\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\bar{a}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}-\bar{b}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\left[a+\mathrm{k}n\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}\left[b+\mathrm{k}m\right]\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

(79)

In this relation, T-duality acts through the change
\left(a,n,\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}\right)\leftrightarrow(b,m,\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}});
it
leaves invariant the spectrum of the left momenta
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}
while flipping the sign of
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}
. Here are some noteworthy features that arise:

•

Ground configurations
: The charge momenta
(\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}})_{a}
and
(\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}})_{b}
of the ground
configurations span a k- dimensional space. The associated Hilbert space can
be generated by classical ground plane waves
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}_{a}=e^{i\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}_{a}.\boldsymbol{\mathring{x}}}
and
\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}_{b}=e^{i\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{a}.\boldsymbol{\mathring{y}}}
with position variables
\boldsymbol{\mathring{x}}=\mathring{x}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathring{y}}=\mathring{y}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}.}
These waves combine into 2j+1 components with label
-j\leq a\leq j

forming an SU(2) representation of isospin

j=\frac{\mathrm{k}-1}{2}

(80)

which for k=1 gives an isosinglet with j=0 and for k=2, a
doublet with j=1/2.

•

Interpolating vacua
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}_{a}\pm\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}_{b}
: these
describe correlated ground configurations corresponding to the
states with
n=m=0.
For these values, the eqs(
75
) reduce to

\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{l}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(a\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}+b\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{r}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(a\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}-b\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

(81)

They yield the momenta of the k
2
ground configurations
\Psi_{a,b}^{\pm}\sim{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}_{a}\pm\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}_{b}
which are characterised by
the topological index:

\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=2\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}\mathring{p}}\qquad,\qquad 2\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}\mathring{p}}=\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}ab\in 2\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}

(82)

•

Excitations:
For non vanishing
n
and
m,
one obtains
excited states
\Psi_{a,b}^{(n,m)}\sim{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}_{a}^{(n)}\pm\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}_{b}^{(m)}
with relative momenta as follows

\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}

=

\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{k}}{2}}\left(n\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}+m\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}

=

\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{k}}{2}}\left(n\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}-m\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\right)

(83)

and associated topological index

\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=\left(2\mathrm{k}\right)nm\in 2\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z}

(84)

For future reference, it is useful to express the difference
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}
in terms of
the variable
\boldsymbol{H}=\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}+\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}\right)/\sqrt{2}
and
\boldsymbol{P}=\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}\right)/\sqrt{2}
like

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=2\boldsymbol{HP}

(85)

By substituting
\boldsymbol{H}=\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}+\boldsymbol{h}

and
\boldsymbol{P}=\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}+\boldsymbol{p}
, the
topological index splits into three contributions:

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=2\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}\mathring{p}}+2(\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}p}+\boldsymbol{h\mathring{p})}+2\boldsymbol{hp}

(86)

4.1.2
About the even integer condition

Here, we revisit the even integer condition defining the lattices
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r,r}}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{r,r}}
of the discriminant group
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}
. Our formulation involves
three
constraint relations
stemming from the decomposition (
86
) which also
reads

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=(\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{r}}}^{2})+(\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}^{2})+\left[(\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}^{2})+(\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{r}}}^{2})\right]

(87)

It reduces to the familiar even integer condition in the case of a trivial
code with k=1 where
\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}_{\text{{l}/{r}}}=0.
The final result we seek to retain are given by
the conditions (
91
).

To that purpose, we start from the standard even integer condition
of Narain lattices namely

\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}\text{ },\text{ }\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{1}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{r,r}}

(88)

This corresponds to the case where
a=b=0
for which the momenta
\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}},\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}
are
given by (
83
) with CS level k=1 in the action (
B.1
). Using (
83
), one finds
\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=2\mathrm{k}nm
which indeed belongs to 2
\mathbb{Z}
for k=1. For higher values of the CS level, this generalises to 2
\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z}
; i.e:

\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}\in 2\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z}\qquad for\qquad\mathrm{k\geq 1}

(89)

To derive the conditions for non trivial values of the labels (a,b), we
compute
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}
by using (
79
) which yields
\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}(a+\mathrm{k}n)(b+\mathrm{k}m)
. This expression decomposes as in (
72
)
namely
\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}ab+2\left(am+bn\right)+2\mathrm{k}nm.
Given
that
\left(a,b\right)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}
and
\left(n,m\right)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
, it
follows that

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}ab$&$\in$&$\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}\mathbb{Z}$\\
$2\mathrm{k}nm$&$\in$&$2\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$2am$&$\in$&$2\mathbb{Z}$\\
$2bn$&$\in$&$2\mathbb{Z}$\end{tabular}

(90)

However, equating with (
86
), indicates that
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}^{2}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}^{2}=2\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}\mathring{p}}+2(\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}p}+\boldsymbol{h\mathring{p})}+2\boldsymbol{hp}
as in eq(
86
). Accordingly, we deduce
the three constraint relations that characterise the code CFT

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$2\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}\mathring{p}}$&$\in$&$\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}\mathbb{Z}$\\
$2\boldsymbol{hp}$&$\in$&$2\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$2\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}p}$&$\in$&$2\mathbb{Z}$\\
$2\boldsymbol{h\mathring{p}}$&$\in$&$2\mathbb{Z}$\end{tabular}

(91)

Moreover, using the identifications
\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}\mathring{p}}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}ab
and
\boldsymbol{hp}=\mathrm{k}nm
, it also results
the two following:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the structure of the
lattices hosting the ground momenta (
\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}},\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}
) which are given by

\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}$&$=$&$xa\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$&$\in$&$x${R}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}$&$=$&$yb\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}$&$\in$&$y${W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}a\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$&$\in$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}${R}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}b\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$&$\in$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}${R}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\end{tabular}

(92)

with the property
xy=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}.
This condition can be
satisfied in multiple ways such as
x=y=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}
or
x=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}
and
y=1
. For the second case, we have
\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}a\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}\in
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}=b\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}\in
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}

with
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=}\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
Regarding the excited momenta (
\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{p}
), we obtain

\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\boldsymbol{h}$&$=$&$zn\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$&$\in$&$z${R}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}$&$=$&$tm\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}$&$\in$&$t${W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\boldsymbol{h}$&$=$&$n\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}$&$\in$&{R}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}$&$=$&$\mathrm{k}m\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}$&$\in$&$\mathrm{k}${W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}$\end{tabular}

(93)

with
zt=\mathrm{k}
which can be solved for
z=t=\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}
or
equivalently
z=1
and
t=\mathrm{k}
. For the CS level
\mathrm{k=2}
, eq(
92
) reduces to

\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}$&$=$&$\frac{a}{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$&$=$&$\frac{a}{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}$&$=$&$b\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$&$=$&$\frac{b}{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$\boldsymbol{h}$&$=$&$n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$&$=$&$n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}$&$=$&$\mathrm{2}m\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$&$=$&$m\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$\end{tabular}

(94)

The graphic representation of (
\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}},\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}
) within the unit cell of the 2D lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{2}^{\mathrm{1,1}}
is depicted in Figure

. The ground

and the 4 right moving (

.

4.2
Particles in the SU(3) based model

The properties of particle states in the code CFT based on SU(3), with
discriminant group
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}

and lattices (
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}
) realised in terms of the root
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and weight
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
systems, follow an analogous structure to that of the
SU(2) theory. Here, the momenta of the particle states

P_{\text{{l}}/\text{{r}}}:=\left(P_{\text{{l}}/\text{{r}}}\right)_{\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}}^{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}}

(95)

are labeled by two pairs of integer vectors:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
a
pair
(\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b})
with
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},a_{2})
and
\mathbf{b}=(b_{1},b_{2})
ranging in
a_{i},b_{i}=0,...,\mathrm{k}-1
; they label the
ground configurations.
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
A pair
(\mathbf{n,m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
with
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2})

and
\mathbf{m}=(m_{1},m_{2})
determining the degrees of excitations of the
KK and the windings modes.

4.2.1
Momenta
P_{L/R}
and SU(3) symmetry

To derive the left/right momenta
P_{\text{{l}}/\text{{r}}}

for the SU(3) based model, we extend the relation (
79
) from the SU(2)
theory. For that, we start by
\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}/{r}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\boldsymbol{H}\pm\boldsymbol{P)}
where

\boldsymbol{H}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}+\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}\right)\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{P}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}-\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}\right)

(96)

and

\boldsymbol{H}=\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}+\boldsymbol{h\hskip 22.99988pt,\hskip 22.99988ptP}=\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}+\boldsymbol{p}

(97)

here
(\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}},\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}})
encode the
ground configurations while
(\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{p})
are
used to describe the KK and winding excitations. Then, we require the SU(2)
conditions (
91
) to remain valid for the SU(3) model. These constraints
generalise as follows

\begin{tabular}[]{lllcl}$\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}.\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\mathbf{a}.\mathbf{b}$&$\in$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\mathbb{Z}$\\
$\boldsymbol{h}.\boldsymbol{p}$&$=$&$\mathrm{k}\mathbf{n.m}$&$\in$&$\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lllcl}$\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}.\boldsymbol{p}$&$=$&$\mathbf{a.m}$&$\in$&$\mathbb{Z}$\\
$\boldsymbol{h}.\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}$&$=$&$\mathbf{b.n}$&$\in$&$\mathbb{Z}$\end{tabular}

(98)

where each pairing, like
\mathbf{a.b,}
stands for the scalar products,
a_{1}b_{1}+a_{2}b_{2}
. A solution to these constraints (
98
) is given
as follows:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

values of
(\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}},\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}):

\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}

=

\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\left(a_{1}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+a_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}\right)

\in

\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}

=

b\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{1}+b_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{2}

\in

W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

(99)

with

\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}=\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{k}}{3}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}

(100)

Using the relations
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{1}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\left(2\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}\right)
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{2}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\left(\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+2\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}\right),
we also have

\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}

=

\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\left(a_{1}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+a_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}\right)

\in

\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}

=

\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}\left(b_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+b_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}\right)

\in

\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

(101)

where we have set
b_{1}^{\prime}=2b_{1}+b_{2}
and
b_{2}^{\prime}=b_{1}+2b_{2}.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

values of
(\boldsymbol{h},\boldsymbol{p}):

\boldsymbol{h}

=

n_{1}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+n_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}

\in

R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

\boldsymbol{p}

=

\mathrm{k}\left(m_{1}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{1}+m_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{2}\right)

\in

\mathrm{k}
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

(102)

reading also like

\boldsymbol{h}

=

n_{1}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+n_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}

\in

R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

\boldsymbol{p}

=

m_{1}^{\prime}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}+m_{2}^{\prime}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}

\in

R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

(103)

with
m_{1}^{\prime}=2m_{1}+m_{2}
and
m_{2}^{\prime}=m_{1}+2m_{2}
.

By substituting these expressions into (
96
), we get

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{H}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(a^{i}+\mathrm{k}n^{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}$\\
$\boldsymbol{P}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(b_{i}+\mathrm{k}m_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}$\end{tabular}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{H}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\bar{a}^{i}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}$\\
$\boldsymbol{P}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\bar{b}_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}$\end{tabular}

(104)

and subsequently

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{l}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\bar{a}^{i}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}+\bar{b}_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left[\left(a^{i}+\mathrm{k}n^{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}+\left(b_{i}+\mathrm{k}m_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}\right]

\boldsymbol{P}_{\text{{r}}}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left(\bar{a}^{i}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}-\bar{b}_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{k}}}\left[\left(a^{i}+\mathrm{k}n^{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}-\left(b_{i}+\mathrm{k}m_{i}\right)\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}\right]

(105)

We conclude by giving the locii of the ground states characterised by the
pair (

) written in
terms of the
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}
as in eq(

). Focussing on
\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}},
the loci for the cases k=2 and k=3 are depicted

4.2.2
Ground configurations and SU(3) representations

We begin by recalling that, for SU(2) based model, the ground state
configurations for a CS level k transform in the representation with isospin

j=(k-1)/2
as in (
80
). In the case of code CFTs based on SU(3), a
similar structure emerges. By using
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{1}=\frac{\mathrm{k}}{3}(2\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{1}-\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{2})
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{2}=\frac{\mathrm{k}}{3}(2\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{2}-\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{1}),
the scaled quantum numbers
(\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}^{\prime})=(\sqrt{3\mathrm{k}}\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}},\sqrt{\mathrm{k}/3}\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}})
characterising the ground configurations are given by the
weight vectors

\boldsymbol{\mathring{h}}^{\prime}

=

r_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}+r_{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}

\boldsymbol{\mathring{p}}^{\prime}

=

b_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}+b_{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}

(106)

where the integers
r_{1}=2a_{1}-a_{2}
and
r_{2}=2a_{2}-a_{1}
(modulo k) take values in the range
\left[0,k-1\right].
These quantum
numbers correspond to the highest weight vectors of SU(3). Recall
that highest weight representations
\mathcal{D}^{(s_{1},s_{2})}
of the
symmetry SU(3) are defined by the highest weight vector
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}=s_{1}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{1}+s_{2}\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{2}
parameterised by two positive integers
\left(s_{1},s_{2}\right).
Their
dimensions are given by

d_{s_{1},s_{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(s_{1}+1\right)\left(s_{2}+1\right)\left(s_{1}+s_{2}+2\right)

(107)

For the particular situation
s_{2}=0
where the
SU(3)
is broken down to
SU(2)\times U(1),
the dimension reduces to
d_{s,0}=\frac{1}{2}\left(s+1\right)\left(s+2\right).
As for the case
s=2j,
we have
d_{2j,0}=\left(2j+1\right)\left(j+1\right)
that splits into
j+1

multiplets like

d_{2j,0}=\underbrace{\left(2j+1\right)+...+\left(2j+1\right)}_{j+1\text{
iso-multiplets}}

(108)

5
Lattice fermions and topological phases

This section builds on the previous and further
develops the connection between Narain CFT and topological matter with Dirac
cones formulated in terms of fermionic lattices. To establish this link
explicitly, we present three
guiding arguments
(
A1
,
A2
and
A3
) while focussing on
the CS level k=2 to illustrate the basic ideas using the associated 2D
lattices
R
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
:

•

A1
:
lattice

W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
=
honeycomb

\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{2}}+\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{2}}
.

The weight lattice
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
contains the
root lattice
R
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\subset
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
leading to the discriminant
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}/\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{2},
this allows to split the weight lattice as
follows

\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\ \ \mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{2}}\ \ \mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\text{ \ }\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{2}}\quad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{2}}$&$=$&$\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$\\
$\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{2}}$&$=$&$\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\backslash\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$\end{tabular}

(109)

To distinguish the two sublattices of
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
, we introduce a physical hypothesis: we interpret the
lattices
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{2}}
and
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{2}}
as being
occupied by different (
colored
) particles, each localized on one
sublattice as described in
A2
.

•

A2
:
the KK/W correspondence

Leveraging the root/weight duality and the KK

\leftrightarrow

Winding correspondence, we propose that quantum excitations
\left|{\small KK}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\rangle
sit in
\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{2}}
while the winding modes
\left|{\small W}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\rangle
live in
\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{2}}
; see Figure
15

for illustration.

Figure 15:
Lattice of KK and winding particles. Each unit cell contains a pair
KK/W in agreement with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry of code CFT at CS level
k=2.

This assumption implies that both type of states
\left|{\small KK}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\rangle
and
\left|{\small W}_{\mathbf{m}}\right\rangle
are hosted by the honeycomb
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
where each unit cell is occupied by a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

doublet as also depicted in Figure
19
),

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\left|{\small KK}\right\rangle\\
\left|{\small W}\right\rangle\end{array}\right)

(110)

Observe that the NCFT based on
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

has a central anomaly
c_{\text{{l/r}}}=2
which suggests that the
theory has two bosonic conformal fields
X^{1}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
and
X^{2}({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}})
splitting into left
X_{L}^{i}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}\right)
and right
