moving fields.
In complex coordinates
z={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}+i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}},
these become the holomorphic
X_{L}^{i}=X_{L}^{i}\left(z\right)
and the anti-holomorphic

•

A3
:
fermionisation

We apply fermionisation techniques
5

i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 269$}}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\partial_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 295$}}}}=\mathbf{0}

with

here,

. Due to the homogeneity of the honeycomb lattice, the coupling

)
,

5.2
Dirac cones and gapless states

In this subsection, we consider the gapless regime by setting the mass and
diagonal couplings to
M=t_{a}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 288$}}}}=t_{a}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}=0
in order
to derive the location of the Dirac cones by computing the zero modes of
\mathbb{H}_{lat}^{(1)}
. To that purpose, observe first that
f_{\mathbf{k}}

is invariant under cyclic permutation (
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{2},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{3}
) generating
\mathbb{Z}_{3};
and as such,
f_{\mathbf{k}}
can be also factorised in two equivalent ways as follows:

f_{\mathbf{k}}=te^{i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{2}}\left(1+e^{i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}}+e^{-i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}}\right),\qquad f_{\mathbf{k}}=te^{i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{3}}\left(1+e^{-i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}}+e^{-i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{3}}\right)

(132)

The two energy eigenvalues E
±
of the matrix
\mathbb{H}_{lat}
are
given by
\pm\sqrt{f_{\mathbf{k}}f_{\mathbf{k}}^{\ast}}
from which we
deduce the expression of the gap energy which is equal to
E_{g}=2\sqrt{f_{\mathbf{k}}f_{\mathbf{k}}^{\ast}}.
Hence, the condition for a vanishing
gap is given by
f_{\mathbf{k}}=0
; its solution can be inspired from the
well known identity
1+e^{i\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}}+e^{i\frac{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}}=0

concerning the three roots of
z^{3}=1.
It leads to two different solutions
(mod
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
transformations), one at
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}
and the other at
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}^{\ast}
. They are
often termed as the Dirac points and are given by the particular momenta

(133)

or equivalently
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{\mathbf{m}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(m_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}+m_{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right)
with

Additionally, we have

\hline\cr$\ \ \mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}^{\ast}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$\ \ \frac{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}$ $\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}$\\
\hline\cr$\ \ \mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}^{\ast}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{3}$&$=$&$\ \ \frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}$ $\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits 2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}$\\
\hline\cr\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}^{\ast}$&$=$&$\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}\left(2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right)$\\
&$=$&$\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}$\end{tabular}

(134)

or equivalently
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{\mathbf{m}}^{\ast}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}^{\ast}+2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(m_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+m_{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)
giving the Brillouin zone. The Dirac points together with
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
symmetry are depicted by the Figure
16
.

Figure 16:
Dirac points
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}^{\ast}
as well as the action do the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}

transformations.

Near these Dirac points; say
\mathbf{k={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}_{0}+q}
with small
\mathbf{q},
the function
f
approximates at leading order like
3te^{i\frac{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{6}}\mathbf{q.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}
. Putting into
f_{\mathbf{k}}=te^{i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{2}}\left(1+e^{i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}}+e^{-i\mathbf{k.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}}\right)
with
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right)
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 289$}}}}_{2}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}},
then using
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3},
we get

f_{\mathbf{q}}

\simeq

te^{i\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}}\left(\mathbf{q.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}\right)+te^{-i\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 276$}}}}_{0}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}}\left(\mathbf{q.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)+O\left(\mathbf{q}^{2}\right)

\simeq

-\frac{t}{2}\mathbf{q.}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)+i\frac{t\sqrt{3}}{2}\mathbf{q.}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)+O\left(\mathbf{q}^{2}\right)

(135)

Substituting in the hamiltonian (
123
) while setting
f_{\mathbf{q}}:=q_{x}-iq_{y}+O\left(q^{2}\right)
with
q_{x}=-\frac{t}{2}\mathbf{q.}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}\right)
and
q_{y}=\frac{t\sqrt{3}}{2}\mathbf{q.}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}\right)
, we end up with

\mathbb{H}_{loc}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&q_{x}-iq_{y}\\
q_{x}+iq_{y}&0\end{array}\right)+O\left(\mathbf{q}^{2}\right)

(136)

where the leading linear term is the Dirac operator
\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{q}}
.

6
Conclusion and discussion

In this study, we began by revisiting the so-called
construction A of code CFTs realising Narain CFTs with an initial emphasis on the lattices (
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{1,1}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathrm{1,1}}
) sitting in
\mathbb{R}^{1,1}
and then extended the analysis to their higher dimensional generalisations
(
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r,r}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{r,r}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathrm{r,r}})
. These lattices are characterised by the central charges of
the 2D NCFT (
c_{\text{{l}}}=c_{\text{{r}}}=\mathrm{r}
) and
the CS level
\mathrm{k}
of the 3D holographic dual. Then, in section 2, we showed that the lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}
and its dual
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathrm{1,1}}
as well as the even self dual
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}
exhibit remarkable properties; in particular the following:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
They can be imagined via the nested structure:

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{2}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1,1}

(137)

with components provided by fibrations of root-like
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
and weight-like
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
lattices of the su(2) algebra, given explicitly by:

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}=\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\times\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}},\qquad\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}=\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\times\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}},\qquad\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast}=\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\times\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}

(138)

We refer to them as root-like and weight-like because only for k=2, the
lattices coincide exactly with the standard root and weight lattices of
su(2). Generally speaking,
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}

consists of points of the form
\{n\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}

,

n\in\mathbb{Z\}}
while
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
is defined by
\{m\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}},}

m

\in\mathbb{Z}\}

with dilated root
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}
and compressed fundamental
weight
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}
related to their standard counterparts
\textstyle\mathchar 267
and
\textstyle\mathchar 277
as

\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}=}\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{k}}{2}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}\hskip 22.99988pt,\hskip 22.99988pt\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

For physical applications, we interpreted the sites
x
{}_{n}\in
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
as hosts of Kaluza-Klein
excitations
\left|KK_{n}\right\rangle
and the sites
k
{}_{m}\in
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\backslash
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
as carriers of winding modes
\left|W_{n}\right\rangle
. A detailed discussion of this description was given
in section 5.

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)
The one dimensional lattices
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}=\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}\mathbb{Z}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}\mathbb{Z}
defined for a generic CS level k, exhibit several notable properties: First, for k=1, they coincide

R
{}_{\mathrm{1}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}=
W
{}_{\mathrm{1}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}.
In this case, the lattice is integral and
self dual with generators obeying
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}^{2}=\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{2}=1
.
Second, for k=2,
R
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}=\sqrt{\mathrm{2}}\mathbb{Z}

correspond to the root lattice of SU(2) and
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{2}}}\mathbb{Z}

is its weight lattice. The former is even integer valued whereas the latter
is half integer. Third, these discrete lattices
R
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
can be identified geometrically as the
(anti)-diagonal 1D sublattices of the ambient
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}

and the
\frac{1}{\mathrm{2}}\mathbb{Z}\times\frac{1}{\mathrm{2}}\mathbb{Z}

square lattices respectively.

(\mathbf{3})
A detailed inspection of these properties leads to the key
identification:
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}=\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{k}}{2}}\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}}\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
where
\textstyle\mathchar 267
and
\textstyle\mathchar 277
are
the usual simple root and the fundamental weight
\textstyle\mathchar 277
vectors
of SU(2) which satisfy
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}=2,

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}=1/2

as well as
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=1.
For generic CS levels k
>
2, the inner products scale like

\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}^{2}=\mathrm{k},\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{2}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}},\qquad\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}.\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=1

From the above description, several important consequences follow:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

there is an embedding hierarchy
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}\subseteq
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
for all
\mathrm{k\geq 1}
. The discriminant
group
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}/
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}=\mathbb{Z}/(\mathrm{k}\mathbb{Z})
, in agreement with the well
known property regarding the group centre of SU(2) implying

W
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}/
R
{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{2}}
.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

For
\mathrm{k>2,}

W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
can be decomposed as a union of k
isomorphic sublattices {
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}\}}
labeled as

\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}=\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\quad,\mathbf{\hskip 22.99988pt}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}=0,...,k-1\mathop{\mathrm{m}od}\nolimits\mathrm{k}

(139)

\left(\mathbf{iii}\right)

Given these
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
, one can then build the triplet
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
following the fibration prescription. For example, the even
self dual lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
is realised as the product
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}\times
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
.
Vector sites
\mathbf{u}_{n,m}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{\mathbf{2}}}
are labeled by two integers with components
n\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}\oplus m\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}
; its Euclidian
pairing
\mathbf{u}_{n,m}^{T}\mathbf{u}_{n,m}
is given by
\mathrm{k}n^{2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}m^{2}
while its Lorentzian product reads as follows

\mathbf{u}_{n,m}^{T}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}u}_{n,m}=2nm\in 2\mathbb{Z}

(140)

The self duality feature of
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}
is
captured by the unimodular property of the matrix
\tilde{A}_{i}^{j}\oplus\tilde{B}_{i}^{j}
which is manifestly ensured by the duality relation
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}.\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=1
. For graphic illustrations of the
discrete structures of the various lattices, see the Figures
2
,
3
,
4
,
5

and
7
.

Moreover, building on the SU(2) results, we constructed new realisations of
Narain CFTs based on the root-like
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and the weight-like
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

lattices. Several structural features established for su(2) carry over
naturally to the SU(3)- based CFT. Particularly, for generic CS levels k, the inclusion
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\subset
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
holds and the
discriminant
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}/
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
group is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}
. As with SU(2), the case of k=3, the symmetry
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{3}}
emerging from the discriminant corresponds to the centre of
SU(3) generated by the 3-cycle
(1,2,3)
acting on the fundamental 3D
representations of SU(3) as given by (
121
).

This SU(3) based construction was initially developed for central charges
c
{}_{L/R}=2
but it generalizes directly to c
{}_{L/R}=2\mathrm{d}
by using higher dimensional self dual lattices
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}(\mathrm{d,d})}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2\mathrm{d},2\mathrm{d}}
with nested structure as:

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}(\mathrm{d,d})}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3(\mathrm{d,d})}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}(\mathrm{d,d})}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2\mathrm{d},2\mathrm{d}}

(141)

For d=1, the three lattices in the above sequence (
141
) correspond
respectively to the fibrations
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
as well as
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
. To illustrate this construction, we gave
several explicit realisations as depicted by the Figures
9
,

10
,
11

and
20
.
The
generalisation to d
>
1 follows straightforwardly.

After establishing this new framework in terms of algebraic data of code
based Narain CFT, we exploited the new setting to develop a pathway towards
topological phases of matter by linking Narain CFT to critical lattice QFT. We set out by describing the particle content of the model by
studying the lattice fields excitations that manifest as KK and winding
modes. We then proposed three guiding arguments based on: (
i
) the structure of the weight lattice, (
ii
) the duality with the
root grid and the correspondence with the KK/winding system in addition to (
iii
) the possibility of fermionising the bosonic excitations to
introduce a tight binding model with Dirac cones. By deriving the
particle states populating the critical lattice points, we were thus able to
capture key features of gapless topological systems akin to that of the
Haldane theory of quantum anomalous Hall effect. This unlocked
several promising research directions among which is the embedding of these
lattice dualities into holographic ones with a one to one correspondence
between the bulk duals. It will be interesting to investigate
whether one can construct a bulk theory to the 2D lattice QFT describing the
topological matter as a realisation of the AB Chern-Simons theory dual to
the code based Narain CFT.

Appendices:

The following three appendices provide technical details omitted from the
main text in order to alleviate and streamline the presentation. In appendix
A, we give a review on the link between code CFTs and Narain ensembles. In
appendix B, we describe construction A of code Narain CFT with explicit
realisations. In appendix C, we complete the analysis of section 3 by
discussing the cases of k
>
3 and k
<
3.

Appendix A
From code-CFT to Narain ensemble

In this appendix, we review the construction of two-dimensional Narain
theories from the perspective of code-based CFTs. We begin by
outlining the key elements of code-CFT constructions and establishing their
correspondence with Narain CFTs through the associated partition functions. We then examine the sequence of lattices these constructions
generate and revisit their interpretation.

A.1 Overview on code-CFT

Over the past decade, an increasing interest has been devoted to the
connection between additive codes and a class of Narain conformal field
theories (NCFT) with U(1)
{}^{\mathrm{c}_{\text{{l}}}}\times

. We also have the following
result:

uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}=\mathrm{k(}uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}})=\mathrm{k}^{2}(uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{2}})

(A.149)

Regarding new realisations of construction A of code CFT going beyond the
SU(2) buildings briefly presented above, we extend the SU(2) realisation (
A.147
) to higher dimensional lattices of Lie algebras especially those
symmetries with rank 2 such as SU(3), SO(5) and G2. Focussing on the
interesting case of SU(3), we get for the 4D lattices involved in the basis
sequence
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}
hosted by
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{2},\mathrm{2}}
the following representation

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$\\
$\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times${W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$\\
$\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&{W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times${W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}<uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}<uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

(A.150)

where for k=3, the 2D discrete sets
R
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and
W
{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
sitting in
\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{2}}
are nothing but the root and the weight lattices of SU(3).
For this generalisation, we distinguish three cases according to the values
of the Chern-Simons level k of the holographic dual of the Narain
CFT.

b) Three sectors

These cases concern the following intervals:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

Case k=3
: this is the canonical
CS level for which the discriminant group is equal to
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{3}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{3}}
. Here, the abelian set
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{3}}
is just the group centre of SU(3). For this value, we find that the 4D lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
is
made of the fibration of two hexagonal lattices like
R
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times
R
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
while
the dual
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}
is
constructed out of the fibration of two triangular lattices as
W
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\times
W
{}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
. The areas of the unit cells of these lattices are depicted by the
Figure
18

in yellow color where for convenience we have also
exhibited the triangulation of the underlying surfaces.

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$uc\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\sqrt{3}$\\
$uc${W}${}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$3$\\

(A.151)

showing that

and then
uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=9\times uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}.
In these relations the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{i}
’s are the
two fundamental weight vectors of SU(3) with angle
(\widehat{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}})=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{6}
and the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
’s are the associated simple roots with
(\widehat{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}})=\frac{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}{3}.
In the Figure

, the representative graphs have 18 fundamental elementary

).

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

Case k
>

: the

and

For instance, the group
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}
indicates that

{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
is made of the superposition of

\mathbb{A}_{0}\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\mathbb{A}_{1}\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\cdots\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{k-2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\bigcup}\mathbb{A}_{\mathrm{k-1}}

(A.152)

each one of them is isomorphic to

{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

but distant by the weight vectors
\textstyle\mathchar 290
\textstyle\mathchar 277
with
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}=0,...,\mathrm{k}-1\mathrm{.}
We also have the following
relationships between the values of the areas of the unit cells of the real

{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

uc\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\frac{\mathrm{k}^{2}}{3}\times uc\text{{W}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=\frac{\mathrm{k}^{4}}{9}\times uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}

(A.153)

They can be checked by using the results

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$uc\text{{R}}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}}{\sqrt{3}}$\\
$uc${W}${}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\frac{\sqrt{3}}{\mathrm{k}}$\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\frac{\mathrm{k}^{2}}{3}$\\
$uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}$&$=$&$\frac{3}{\mathrm{k}^{2}}$\end{tabular}

(A.154)

from which we also learn
uc\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{3}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}=1
independently of the value of the CS level.

\left(\mathbf{iii}\right)

Case k
<
3
: for

\mathrm{k}=1,2
. While the CS level k=1 is somehow

{}_{\mathrm{1}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}<uc

. This condition holds only for k
\geq\sqrt{3}
coming from solving

. This bound rules out k=1 but includes k=2 for which
the weight lattice

the 2D honeycomb while the

{}_{\mathrm{2}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
is an

.

{}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}.

Appendix B
Construction A of code Narain CFT

Following
1B
;
1C
, the
construction A of code based NCFT establishes a correspondence between
algebraic coding theory on the finite abelian group
G_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r,r}}\sim\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r}}
and CFTs defined on toroidal backgrounds. This framework
provides a systematic method to build Narain CFTs, describing strings on
torii, by using code theory based on the Mac-Williams functional W
{}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(X\right)

21
;
22
. The structure of linear
codes
\mathcal{C}\subset G_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r,r}}
determine the
fundamental features of the associated NCFTs. In the holographic dual of
these NCFTs
23
, the positive integer k corresponds to the
Chern-Simons (CS) level of the so-called AB theory which consists of two
gauge blocks of abelian potentials
\mathcal{A}:=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{r}}\mathcal{A}^{i}Q_{i}
and
\mathcal{B}:=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathrm{r}}\mathcal{B}_{i}\tilde{Q}^{i}
valued in [U(1)
{}_{A}\times
U(1)
{}_{B}]^{\mathrm{r}}
and
coupled as follows

\mathcal{S}_{\text{{cs}}}+\mathcal{S}_{\text{{bnd}}}=\frac{i\mathrm{k}}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\mathcal{M}}Tr\left(\mathcal{A}\wedge d\mathcal{B}+\mathcal{B}\wedge d\mathcal{A}\right)+\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{\partial\mathcal{M}}Tr\left(\mathcal{A}_{z}\mathcal{A}_{\bar{z}}+\mathcal{B}_{z}\mathcal{B}_{\bar{z}}\right)

(B.1)

with trace relations as
Tr(Q_{i}\tilde{Q}^{j})={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}
and
Tr(Q_{i}Q_{j})=K_{ij}
as well as
Tr(\tilde{Q}^{i}\tilde{Q}^{j})=\tilde{K}^{ij}.
In the eq(
B.1
),
\mathcal{M}
is generally a 3D handlebody
(e.g. a solid torus) with boundary
\partial\mathcal{M}
given by
a Riemann surface
\Sigma_{g}
such as the 2-torus for genus
g=1
. For the
leading value of the integral central charge
c_{\text{{l}/{r}}}=1
, corresponding to the model r=1, the code-CFT constructed
over
G_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}:=G_{\mathrm{k}}
maps codes
\mathcal{C}

into even self dual lattices
\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}
constrained like

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathrm{1,1}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{1,1}

(B.2)

In this hierarchy, the discrete set
\mathbf{\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}:=\Lambda}
is a 2D even integral lattice
embedded in the Lorentzian plane
\mathbb{R}^{1,1}
endowed with the metric:

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)\qquad\leftrightarrow\qquad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{ij}\equiv\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0_{AA}&1_{AB}\\
1_{BA}&0_{BB}\end{array}\right)

(B.3)

and scalar product
<\mathbf{v,v}>_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}}=\mathbf{v}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{v}

evaluating to
2v_{1}v_{2}.
The metric
\textstyle\mathchar 273
couples the two gauge
sectors
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
in eq(
B.1
). The dual lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast}
is defined as the set

\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast}=\left\{\mathbf{w}\text{ }|\text{ }\mathbf{v}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{Z},\text{ }\forall\mathbf{v\in}\Lambda\right\}

(B.4)

where
\mathbf{\Lambda}=\left\{\mathbf{v}\text{ }|\text{ }\mathbf{v}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{v}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\right\}
is an even integer lattice
satisfying the usual inclusion property
\mathbf{\Lambda}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast}.
The intermediate lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}
is both even and self dual,

that is
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}\sim\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\ast},
and is nested between
\mathbf{\Lambda}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast};
see Figure
17
for illustration.

B.1 An explicit realisation

Here, we give an explicit realisation of the triplet (
\mathbf{\Lambda},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast}
) which has been
exploited in our analysis, sections 2 & 3, where it has been given a
natural interpretation in terms of the root and the weight lattices of SU(2)
and SU(3). To gain further insight into the embedding chain
\mathbf{\Lambda}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}\subset\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast},

we start by describing the sites
\mathbf{v}
populating the abelian lattice

\mathbf{\Lambda}.
They are represented by discrete 2D vectors
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}=v_{\mathbf{n}}^{i}\mathbf{e}_{i}
labeled by:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the integer vector
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2})
parameterising the
square lattice
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
with unit spacing
a
=1;
in addition to
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the usual canonical basis
vectors
\mathbf{e}_{1}=(\mathtt{a},0)
and
\mathbf{e}_{2}=(0,\mathtt{a})

with Euclidian
\mathbf{e}_{1}.\mathbf{e}_{2}=0
but Lorentzian
\mathbf{e}_{1}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{e}_{2}=\mathtt{a}^{2}=1.
For convenience, we express the
vector
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}
as a linear combination of the integer
vector
\mathbf{n}
like

v_{\mathbf{n}}^{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\Lambda_{j}^{i}n^{j}\qquad,\qquad\Lambda_{j}^{i}=\frac{\partial v_{\mathbf{n}}^{i}}{\partial n^{j}}\qquad,\qquad\Lambda:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&b\\
c&d\end{array}\right)

(B.5)

where
\Lambda_{j}^{i}
is a characteristic matrix of the real lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda},
acting as a linear map between
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
\mathbf{\Lambda}
with
\det\Lambda_{ij}\neq 0
(i.e:
\Lambda_{ij}:\mathbf{n}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}\in\mathbf{\Lambda}
). For any lattice vector
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}\in\mathbf{\Lambda},
the Lorentzian scalar product
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}
must be an even integer (i.e. belongining to 2
\mathbb{Z}
) thus imposing a strong constraint on the form of the generating
matrix
\Lambda_{j}^{i}
. Indeed, substituting
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}=\Lambda.\mathbf{n}
into the Lorentzian scalar yields the conditions

\mathbf{n}^{T}\left(g_{\Lambda}\right)\mathbf{n}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\text{ }\qquad with\qquad g_{\Lambda}=\Lambda^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\Lambda

(B.6)

where (
i
) the induced metric
g_{\Lambda}
is a quadratic function
of
\Lambda
, with (
ii
) diagonal entries taking even integer values

g_{\Lambda}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2m&l\\
l&2n\end{array}\right),\qquad m,n,l\in\mathbb{Z}

(B.7)

this implies that
\det g_{\Lambda}=-\det\Lambda^{2}.
Additionally, the
characteristic matrix
\Lambda
is not unique; it is defined up to
\mathcal{O}
(1,1,
\mathbb{R}
) orthogonal transformations namely
\Lambda^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}\Lambda
with
\mathcal{O}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathcal{O}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}.
A simple example of

such transformation is given by
\mathcal{O}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}
leading to
\Lambda^{\prime}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\Lambda.
Moreover, using (
B.5
), we obtain the relations
ac=m,

bd=n
and
ad+bc=l
along with the identity
4mn-l^{2}=-\left(ad-bc\right)^{2}
that
factorises like

\det g_{\Lambda}=\left(m+n+\sqrt{l^{2}+\left(m-n\right)^{2}}\right)\left(m+n-\sqrt{l^{2}+\left(m-n\right)^{2}}\right)

(B.8)

In what follows, we exhibit the dependence on the CS level
\mathrm{k}
by
labeling the above 2D lattice triplet like
\left(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast}\right)
with (prime) integer
\mathrm{k}\geq 2
. These
lattices can be realised in terms of scaled cubic lattices with spacings
a=\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}
and
a_{\ast}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}
as follows

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}

\sim

\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}

\mathbb{Z}

\mathbb{\times}

\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}

\mathbb{Z}

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast}

\sim

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}

\mathbb{Z}

\times

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}

\mathbb{Z}

\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}

\sim

\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}

\mathbb{Z}

\mathbb{\times}

\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}

\mathbb{Z}

(B.9)

Formally, the intermediate lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}
may be imagined as the product
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{k}}\times\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast}
where
\mathbf{R}_{\mathrm{k}}
and
\mathbf{W}_{\mathrm{k}}
correspond, respectively, to the root and weight
lattices as defined in section 2. The realisation (
9
) satisfies the
following features:
\left(\mathbf{1}\right)
each site
v_{\mathbf{n}}^{i}
generating the real lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}
can be
written as
\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}X_{j}^{i}n^{j}
with invertible

\Lambda_{j}^{i}=\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}\text{ }X_{j}^{i}

(B.10)

and induced
g_{\Lambda}=\mathrm{k}X^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}X
which must have the typical
form (
B.7
). A particular solution for
X
is given by the identity
matrix
X=I_{2\times 2}
. For this special solution, the induced metric
becomes
g_{\Lambda}=\mathrm{k}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}
which corresponds to setting
m=n=0

and
l=\mathrm{k}
in (
B.7
). Substituting into the product
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}},
we obtain
n^{i}\left(g_{\Lambda}\right)_{ij}n^{j}=2\mathrm{k}n^{1}n^{2}
which is indeed an
even integer.
\left(\mathbf{2}\right)
The sites
\mathbf{w}_{\mathbf{m}}

in the dual
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast}
have components
w_{\mathbf{m}}^{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}Y_{j}^{i}m^{j}
with

\left(\Lambda^{\ast}\right)_{j}^{i}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}Y_{j}^{i}

(B.11)

Inserting this into the form
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{n}}^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\mathbf{w}_{\mathbf{m}},
while requiring the duality constraint, we end up with the
condition
X^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}Y=n

\in

\mathbb{Z}
; this is easily satisfied by
choosing
Y=(X^{T}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}})^{-1}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}.
Therefore, for the particular solution

X=I_{2\times 2}
and
Y={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}
, we have the characteristic matrices (
\Lambda,\Lambda^{\ast}
) and the associated induced metrics (
g_{\Lambda},g_{\Lambda^{\ast}}
) explicitly given by

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\Lambda$&$=$&$\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}I_{2\times 2}$\\
$g_{\Lambda}$&$=$&$\mathrm{k}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\Lambda^{\ast}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}$\\
$g_{\Lambda^{\ast}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}$\end{tabular}

(B.12)

with determinants
\det\Lambda=\mathrm{k}
,
\det\Lambda^{\ast}=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}}
and
\det g_{\Lambda}=-\mathrm{k}^{2}
as well as
\det g_{\Lambda^{\ast}}=-\frac{1}{\mathrm{k}^{2}}
. For this specific solution,
one notes that
\Lambda^{-1}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\Lambda^{\ast}
highlighting the fact
that
\Lambda^{\ast}
is closely related to the inverse
\Lambda^{-1}.

Based on these results, we deduce the explicit forms of the intermediate
lattice
\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}
, its dual
\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}\sim\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}
and the
corresponding induced metrics
g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}}
and
g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}}
as follows

\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}\end{array}\right),\qquad\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}&0\end{array}\right),\qquad g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

(B.13)

They verify the property
\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}=I_{2\times 2}
and are unimodular in the
sense that
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}=1
,
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast}=-1
and
\det g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}}=-1.
This unimodularity captures the self duality property of the
intermediate lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}.

B.2 Higher dimensions

Using the results obtained for the case r=1, the construction A for
code-CFTs with higher central charges
c_{\text{{l}/{r}}}=\mathrm{r\geq 2}
extends straightforwardly by the help of tensor sums and
products. In this generalisation, the codes
\mathcal{C}
are embedded in the abelian group
\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{r}}
and are associated with a 2r
dimensional lattice
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}
constrained as follows

\underbrace{\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}\oplus...\oplus\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\mathrm{1,1}}}_{\mathrm{r}\text{ {times}}}\text{ }\subset\text{ }\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}\text{ }\subset\text{ }\underbrace{\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathrm{1,1}}\oplus...\oplus\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}}^{\ast\mathrm{1,1}}}_{\mathrm{r}\text{ {times}}}\text{ }\subset\text{ }\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{r},\mathrm{r}}

(B.14)

When the codes
\mathcal{C}
satisfy additional constraints, namely evenness
and self duality conditions, the associated lattices
\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}
are in turn even and self
dual, thus defining a Narain theory with central charge
\mathrm{r}
. For
the particular solution introduced earlier, the relevant matrices take the
form

\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}I_{\mathrm{r}}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}I_{\mathrm{r}}\end{array}\right),\quad\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}I_{\mathrm{r}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathrm{k}}}I_{\mathrm{r}}&0\end{array}\right),\quad g_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{\mathrm{r}}\\
I_{\mathrm{r}}&0\end{array}\right)

(B.15)

with
I_{\mathrm{r}}
referring to the r
\times
r identity matrix. These
matrices satisfy
\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=I_{2{\small\mathrm{r,2r}}},

\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=1
as well as
\det\Lambda_{\mathrm{k}\mathcal{C}}^{\ast{\small\mathrm{r,r}}}=\left(-\right)^{r}
.

Appendix C
Lattices
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}},\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\ast\mathbf{su}_{3}}
for
k
<
3 and k
>
3

) for values of the CS level k distinct
than k=3. Particularly, we construct models with k
>
3 and then
discuss cases having k
<
3.

The extension of the root

C.2 Lattice triplet for

k
<
3

An intriguing case arises when considering CS levels k
<
3,
particularly for
\mathrm{k}=1
and
\mathrm{k}=2.
Recall that for generic
k, the scaled root and weight lattice generators
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}
obey the relations:
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{j}=\frac{\mathrm{k}}{3}K_{ij}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{j}=\frac{3}{\mathrm{k}}\tilde{K}^{ij}
as well as
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}..\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}
. These imply the squared
lengths
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}^{2}=\frac{2\mathrm{k}}{3}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{i}^{2}=\frac{2}{\mathrm{k}}
that lead to the ratios
\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}_{i}^{2}/\mathbf{\tilde{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}_{i}^{2}
that
exceed one only for
\mathrm{k}\geq\sqrt{3}
as illustrated in the table
below:

lattice topological matter with Dirac points will be reported in a future
